## 14.11 Big data in healthcare

Big data is the processing of massive volume of complex data. It has become a popular trend across the world. It is used to find patterns, analyze, manage the data, and help make better decisions. This technology has been burgeoning in many sectors in the past few years. It involves storing, analyzing, discovering hidden patterns, and intelligently managing datasets by gathering deep insights that help in better functioning of the system. It deals with datasets of the size of petabytes. It analyzes and strategically arranges structured, semistructured, and unstructured datasets. They are incorporated in companies to handle enormous chunks of data both economically and strategically. Healthcare is a data-rich industry with data flowing in from health records, prescriptions, examinations, personal info, and readings. All this data keeps increasing the amount of data in the field at a tremendous rate. As big data deals with collecting, assessing, and managing data, it can hugely take advantage of the data readily available in the medical field. In the modern technological era, data is being constantly generated from various sources and they have reached a point where the present technologies are struggling to handle it. It is not easy to handle such huge chunks of data as it requires machines with very high computational power. Big data techniques can be utilized here to organize and manage this data efficiently and cost-effectively. With the ever-increasing costs of treatment, it has become quite difficult for the lower class to cope up with the expenses of the procedures and medications. The costs of treatment can also be cut down to some extent by introducing big data in the medical sector. All the mental and social factors of the person are studied for the treatment instead of just considering the symptoms and providing treatment with the help of big data, leading to better care and efficient treatment. Personalized care is given to patients having a history of medical conditions in the past as big data takes entire data into consideration for organizing and making decisions. Big data can help in faster drug discovery due to its high computational ability. It develops a better relationship between doctor and patient as it analyzes and gathers insights from appointment data, referrals, and prescriptions. As it deals with the management and organization of health data, it goes through the entire blocks of huge data and recognizes hidden patterns, which can be very essential for better marketing strategies. It is extremely helpful in predicting accurate outcomes quickly, which leads to faster and better patient care and satisfaction. Big data eventually helps in the entire growth of the hospital in terms of care and economically by providing efficient and personalized care.

Prioritizing and assigning importance to data are the major challenges faced by big data. Since the available data is so huge, the analysis becomes difficult to judge if the insights are essential or not, due to which some irrelevant data points are prioritized. This makes it mandatory for the firm to check if the algorithms are efficient enough and have a good ability to sort data before adopting them. Access to data is another challenge faced by big data. Since the data is very sensitive and it is transferred to various organizations for management, it needs to be secure and particular steps must be taken to ensure that this data does not get misplaced or fall into the wrong hands. Noisy data is a major issue when it comes to big data. Since the efficiency of organization and management of data depends on the quality of data itself, the data must be checked thoroughly and any kind of noise or inconsistency should be removed. Patients must be assigned with a unique variable so that it can be easy to identify and match them. Without a unique identifier it becomes a tedious task to match or find a specific patient. Heterogeneous data also has chances of causing issues during the analysis of data, so any kind of mismatches and irregularity should be avoided ([Murdoch Travis et al., 2013](#bodymatter-014.xhtml#b_9783110648195-014_ref_007)).

The activity of the medical expert must be recorded under any circumstances as any missing data might cause unwanted anomalies and result in unclear conclusions. As predictions and management depends on the data, every single activity must be recorded and stored for better functioning of the system. Big data is analyzing the entire gigantic data to recover hidden patterns and to arrange them smartly to get useful insights. For this to take place all the data from the past must be present in digital form to be analyzed, and converting these old written files into digital data is a tedious and time-consuming task. Big data might provide a new and better approach to handle data generated by hospitals and medical centers but not all companies have enough capital to implement it. Addressing these issues will help the big data work fluidly and will produce economical advantage to the firm. It also results in accurate disease prediction, better patient treatment and care, and improved usage of available electronic data, and gives a more holistic approach to the medical procedures ([Basel et al., 2013](#bodymatter-014.xhtml#b_9783110648195-014_ref_001)).

### 14.11.1 Applications of ML using big data in healthcare

In this modern technological era, most of the companies have started digitalizing all the health data. This data is vast as it includes past health records, diagnosis, medical imaging, and rest of the patient-related data. With the help of electronic health records, doctors have easy access to patients’ medical history. These include any kind of past allergies, medications, and results of various tests that are made available to the doctor effortlessly. The treatment can thus be done in lesser time due to the provided previous test results that are used by the algorithm. Using electronic records, doctors have found a significant rise in treatment as it removes any irregularities caused by bad handwriting of doctors. A substantial decrease in time required for the treatment is also noticed because using electronic records eradicates the need for unnecessary tests, as the past test results are readily available. All these eventually resulted in reduced number of errors made in prescribed medication and dose.

Electronic records are also essential for doctors in setting automatic reminders for appointments, to remind about vaccination, and to prevent unusual issues. They are proven to provide better care by facilitating reminders for periodic check-ups and easy communication between doctors and patients. They diminish the amount of paperwork required by providing electric approval and billing. These records are further passed on to the ML firms that employ this information to enable faster retrieval of data and gain important insights. They are also used to predict the onset of an epidemic outbreak helping the public to take preventive measures sooner. Electronic health records also reduce the ever-increasing costs of treatment by finding clever insights among the data it stores. The benefits from health insurance schemes are also improved considerably by incorporating electronic health records in the medical field. Electronic health records also shine in the management area by reducing the amount of time required for billing and prevent any kind of confusion.

Electronic health records provide enormous raw data that is very helpful in prediction and decision making by analyzing the entire data. Unstructured data is often preferred for this, because it can be stored in a kind of format and structured data cannot record complex data with a lot of variations. For instance, we cannot record abnormal data like patients’ emotions, preferences, lifestyle, and other odd factors using structured data formats. It becomes very difficult to understand and organize all these varied information about the patient using a structured format. They need to be taken in an unstructured format and should be combined using ML algorithms to clearly understand the conveyed information. However, the healthcare sector manages to take advantage of this data to buy using unstructured format to gather diverse data and unify it using ML techniques to improve the overall treatment of the patient. It is also very advantageous in the management area, providing better management by eradicating any unnecessary waste of time and preventing any kind of confusion. Eventually, all these lead to a better care with low cost.

Any kind of data that does not follow the predefined norms or framework is considered as unstructured data. Due to the very high quantity of data, often parts of data that do not follow the predefined framework fall into the electronic records. This happens because the data entered is mostly raw and is not constantly checked for abnormalities. The digital files are provided in a clear and logical format for easy understanding for various fields. But when it comes to healthcare, it is not easy as it is challenging to incorporate high-level software and tools. For this we require experts from various domains to work together. Cloud developers make the data collected from different sensors available on the cloud using various software. ML experts implement ML algorithms for these tools that perform the conversion of stored sensor data into knowledge. By incorporating this in the health department, the efficiency of sensing, predicting, storing, and analyzing of data is drastically improved. The unstructured data is annotated, integrated, and visualized in a fixed format for a clear understanding. If the data is not structured, the information extracted from it is uncertain and would not be of much help in healthcare research.

One of the major challenges of big data is heterogeneity of data. The extraction of information becomes quite difficult if the data is heterogeneous. Grid computers with very high computational powers are generally used to deal with analysis of these huge chunks of data. For the storage of this data, cloud computing technologies are employed as they provide reliable resources and services for storing data. Advanced ML and AI techniques are authorized for handling such data and the most popular platforms are Hadoop and Apache Spark ([Sabyasachi et al., 2019](#bodymatter-014.xhtml#b_9783110648195-014_ref_012)).

## 14.12 Drug discovery using ML

Drug discovery and manufacturing is one of the main applications of ML. In the current state, companies are finding the process of discovering a drug a tedious and time-consuming task. Discovery of a drug requires a lot of predictive power with great accuracy as any complications in the final drug might lead to serious conditions of a patient. At present, there are not many technologies with the computational ability to easily coin out a drug. ML is highly useful in this sector as its predictive capability is unmatched among all the available technologies. Unsupervised learning is used to find out hidden patterns that help in taking better decisions. Governments spend millions of dollars every year in search of finding a new drug for improved treatment or for better results. This technology can significantly cut down the expenditure for discovery of drugs.

To discover a drug, initially the companies go on search for a compound that cures the disease by reacting with the target molecules of the body, but in most of the cases, the compound tends to react with other molecules that are not the target and this might result in life-threatening circumstances. Therefore, companies find it difficult to discover a drug for a particular disease due to lack of predictive methods. ML uses its computational and predictive power to find the drug without any dangerous tests.

The drug discovery process is initiated by extracting information from previous tests from different sources and analyzing it. The procedure involves back-and-forth switching between induction and deduction. This results in identification of an accurate hit. The initial steps involve searching for compounds with decent biological activity. This activity is calculated based on the degree of interaction of the compound with the required enzyme or organism. A hit is the earliest compound that shows good amount of activity with a specific target. Generally, hits are found during the screening of chemical data and compounds like plants, fungi, and bacteria. The second step of this process involves the detection of a lead molecule. Any compound that is showing promising potential for the discovery of a new drug that can be used for treating a disease is known as a lead. These hits are used for prediction of disease and the safety of using a compound. After the identification of a lead compound, modifications are done to structure and to obtain desirable compounds with high clinical benefit which deal no harm.

Hits are chemically modified to increase their activity against specific targets and to eradicate any undesirable side effects during the generation process of lead molecules. The compounds formed after these chemical modifications of hits are known as analogues and the entire process is called hit expansion. For this process of hit expansion, medical experts make use of well-known chemical techniques. Experts emphasize on a particular sequence of reactions to c building blocks together that generate the analogues quickly which increase the efficiency. Any compound containing functional group or atom that can effectively interact with an active site of the target is called a building block. The target contains a particular region called the active site that reacts and combines with the compound through interaction forces. In this way, the initial chemical processes take place for the development of a drug.

Finding an enhanced version of an existing drug is relatively easier when compared to the strenuous task of discovering an entirely new drug. This happens because the chemical space consists of compounds of the order of 10^(60). The firms implement ML techniques that refine this space, making it smaller and easier for the algorithms to find a required compound. These ML techniques are useful in all the stages of drug discovery. They help in identification of biological targets, improving efficiency of discovery, analyzing available data, and making better decisions that are vital for the development of the drug. By eliminating human intervention and confusion, ML techniques remove any kind of anomalies or uncertainties helping to provide an improved development of a drug. ML has also proven to considerably increase the efficiency in predicting the routes for molecules to react and providing the final drug with required properties and characteristics.

Incorporating ML for the discovery of a drug has been very beneficial for R&D department. All the past issues like lack of computational power and resources for analysis for enormous datasets, extremely tedious screening of compound with less error, and possible toxic side effects have been completely eliminated with the help of ML. The finance department has also significantly improved after the use of ML as it cuts down the prices of analyzing huge datasets, predicting the right target, and reduces the time required for the accurate development of a new drug.

ML has taken the process of drug discovery to a whole new level. This is done by integration of genomics information, biological characteristics, and the ability to track targets efficiently. IBM Watson is one of these technologies and it has already discovered five new RNA-binding proteins that are used to detect a neurodegenerative disease called amyotrophic lateral sclerosis. ML algorithms operate on the chemical space for the process of development of new drug molecules. Since the chemical space contains a very wide range of compounds, it serves as a platform to increase the probability of finding the required organic molecule. ML algorithms along with predictive analysis tools make the detection target molecules easier and increase the safety by helping in the association of molecules with their target. They optimize the compound to eradicate any type of side effects and increase the quality of desired attributes. ML techniques also considerably decrease the attrition rates and R&D expenditure by only selecting the compounds needed for analysis and excluding analysis of any unnecessary compounds.

The available data is utilized by diverse techniques for selecting profiles of small molecules based on their structural biology. Wherever the structural data is not sufficient, ML techniques come in handy. Algorithms based on data, disease, biological attributes, or structure are used. Before implementing an ML technique for the development of a drug, it should be validated. The techniques are validated to boost up the success rate in drug discovery on application. Synthesis of the selected molecules is the most essential part in the process of development of a drug. Therefore, ML plays a crucial part as it can prioritize the molecules based on the synthesis and can create tools that make the process easier.

The most popular method used by scientists for the synthesis of drug-like compounds is retrosynthesis. The initial step in retrosynthesis involves analyzing the targets extensively and slowly break them down into smaller molecules or building blocks that are capable of being made easily. The next step involves identification of reactions that have the ability of converting these smaller fragments into required targets. This step is very difficult as it is impossible for humans to comprehend all the chemical reactions present and to pick the most suitable reaction. Here, ML techniques are highly beneficial as they can predict the reactions accurately avoiding wastage of time and resources for analyzing irrelevant data. Any kind of failure is totally avoided with the help of these algorithms. Some voids still occur due to unpredictable causes and misunderstanding of the reaction mechanism. As of now, numerous computer-aided organic compound synthesis systems are assisting experts in selecting the mode of synthesis. There is a new ML technology called 3N-MCTS (Monte Carlo Tree Search) that filters out the less important building blocks and picks the best reactions for the targets. The retrosynthesis systems fall behind this technology as it is relatively faster and more efficient. It further eliminates any unnecessary stages and provides viable synthesis routes quicker. This technology might be faster but it is not very flexible and cannot be applied to every aspect of drug development.

Image-based screening is also a recent technology that is used for various applications in both preclinical experiments and drug development. It excels in categorizing small molecules based on their structure and does it efficiently. It is used to detect specific target molecules on the basis of their characteristics. High-throughput imaging is capable of segregating cancer cells to form a group of similar molecules that have similar mechanism of reaction. For this image-based profiling, supervised learning is generally preferred as it is better in classifying single cells into smaller fragments. The training data needs to be refined with the help of predefined parameters for supervised learning techniques. The result and performance of the screening depend on the quality of data.

The major challenge in incorporating ML in drug development is ensuring drug safety. Knowing its effects on humans and foreseeing its side effects is a very complicated task and takes a lot of resources. Active researches are going on to understand the entire effects of a drug and making sure that it is safe to use. Drug development involves clinical trials. These clinical trials are very expensive. The costs of these clinical trials can be reduced by using the knowledge obtained from past clinical trials in the initial stages of drug development. For this ML is employed to utilize the available data from previous research experiments so that it can analyze and use it to predict the exact use and its side effects. ML algorithm should analyze the data from clinical trials that should support the existing biological data. By implementing these in the discovery process of a drug, better outcomes are possible with improved clinical experiments that eventually lead to better treatment and therapies with very less side effects ([Scheeder et al., 2018](#bodymatter-014.xhtml#b_9783110648195-014_ref_013)).

## 14.13 Conclusion and future work

Healthcare sector is growing exponentially every year as millions of patients keep admitting in hospital every second around the world. Implementing a futuristic technology like ML can help this industry in all aspects. It has a great potential in drastically lowering the expenditure on medical industry and provide improved treatment with better care. Majority of companies have already taken steps to incorporate such a beneficial technology to make treatment more patient-oriented.

Implementing ML algorithms significantly increases the communication between doctor and patient, the amount of time a doctor spends with a patient, and speed of treatment and recovery. They are also highly useful in predicting a disease beforehand accurately, discovery of a drug, and organized management of health records and patient data. They considerably reduce the workload of doctors by performing all the mundane tasks of a doctor at inhuman speeds. The increased availability of electronic health data is utilized by ML for training and learning to improve its precision in all of its tasks. It helps the medical experts in understanding the exact risks and side effects of a new medicine. ML opens up a very wide range of possibilities in healthcare out of which all of them can highly benefit us. It is one of those technologies that can permanently change the medical industry, and all major companies must take an initiative to make use of this technology.

# 15 Computational health informatics using evolutionary-based feature selection

Vanaja Ramaswamy Department of Information Science and Technology, College of Engineering Guindy, Anna University, Chennai, Tamil Nadu, India Saswati Mukherjee Department of Information Science and Technology, College of Engineering Guindy, Anna University, Chennai, Tamil Nadu, India 

#### Abstract

This chapter is a recent survey of evolutionary computation (EC)-based feature selection (FS) techniques whose objective is mainly to improve the accuracy of the machine learning algorithm in minimized computation time. The idea is to bring forth the main strengths of EC as a naturally inspired optimization technique for FS in the machine learning process. The modeling of biological and natural intelligence that has made progressive advancements in the recent decade motivates us to review state-of-the-art FS techniques to add more to the area of computational intelligence. Owing to its importance, the use-case in support of health informatics is also exhibited.

Keywords:  Evolutionary Computation,  Feature selection,  Machine learning,  Computational intelligence,  

## 15.1 Introduction

The chapter introduces machine learning techniques with a focus on feature selection (FS) and reviews the recent state-of-the-art FS mechanisms and their types. FS as an essential sub-process in machine learning allows the learning algorithm to train faster and improves the accuracy of the model by choosing the right subset of features ([Dash and Liu, 1997](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_012)). It also results in a better understanding of the data with good generalization capability. The predictive performance is highly improved when a machine learning task is preceded by an effective FS approach. The chapter explains the general notion of filter-based method and wrapper-based method of FS with the differences in their respective approaches. A general FS (filter/wrapper) is readily applicable as long as the number of features available in the data set is small. As we progress to access big data, both in terms of the sample size and the feature size, no more general approach can do well as the search space is very large ([Xue et al., 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_048)). The immediate need is a mechanism that randomly starts with a heuristic and proceeds on with effective moves in the search of the global optimal position. Therefore, the interest of the chapter is to provide an overview of the evolutionary paradigm of FS mechanism and their importance in computational intelligence. Due to its global exploration ability in search, the evolutionary approach of FS namely genetic algorithm (GA), genetic programming (GP), particle swarm optimization (PSO), ant colony optimization (ACO), and others have become popular in the recent past of the research ([De La Iglesia, 2013](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_013)). This chapter categorizes the FS as filter-based evolutionary methods (FBEM), wrapper-based evolutionary methods (WBEM), and hybrid-based evolutionary methods (HBEM). It explains these three categories of FS with a specific combination of the above-mentioned evolutionary approaches based on the relevance of application and the need for implementation. Concerning the time-sensitiveness of the industry, healthcare is considered as an application where specific use-cases one for FBEM and the other for WBEM are discussed.

## 15.2 Machine learning techniques with a focus on feature selection

“Machine learning is a kind of artificial intelligence which provides an automated learning ability for a system by using the experiences gained in the past.” As irrelevant and redundant features can impact the performance of a model negatively ([https://towardsdatascience.com/feature-selection-techniques-in-machine-learning-with-python-f24e7da3f36e](https://towardsdatascience.com/feature-selection-techniques-in-machine-learning-with-python-f24e7da3f36e)), they can be removed before the learning phase of a machine learning cycle.

FS as an efficient pre-processor can be added as an important paradigm to do this to bring improvement both in time and space complexity.

### 15.2.1 Terminologies involved with machine learning

The following are the terminologies involved for machine learning paradigm:

*   *Model*
    
    A model is a hypothetical representation that learns some concepts from the data by using machine learning techniques.
    
*   *Dataset*
    
    The file that contains “n” number of instances (records to be trained) represented in rows and “f” number of features represented as columns.
    
*   *Feature Vector and features*
    
    The individual record of a data set will constitute a feature vector and each entity in the feature vector represents a feature.
    
*   *Target (Label)*
    
    An output label that a record is associated with is called the target. This will apply to supervised learning methods.
    
*   *Training*
    
    A machine learning algorithm is used to generate patterns that result out of the learning model.
    
*   *Prediction*
    
    New data can be applied to the model to make a prediction based on the features of the data.
    

In any machine learning technique, FS mechanism plays an important role to provide the model, good generalization ability, shorter training time, and increased accuracy and also helps in simplifying the model for the better interpretation for users ([Cai et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_005)).

As shown in [Figure 15.1](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_001), the pre-processing of raw data is performed to make the data ready for the FS process. This is followed by the learning phase that uses some effective machine learning algorithms. When there are several machine learning approaches like supervised learning, unsupervised learning, semi-supervised learning, and reinforcement learning, the diagram mainly explains the supervised learning mechanism where the data with its target is used. The evaluation process to measure accuracy and other required metrics can be performed on the test data set. Now the developed and tested model is ready for making new predictions.

![Figure 15.1: Machine learning process.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_001.png)

Figure 15.1: Machine learning process.

## 15.3 General types of feature selection and their differences

FS techniques are in general classified as filter, wrapper, and embedded methods ([Chandrashekar and Sahin, 2014](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_007)).

### 15.3.1 Filter-based FS methods

The filter-based FS approach applies some statistical methods like information gain, correlation coefficient, or others to assign a numeric score to each feature based on which feature ranking will be performed. This feature ranking can be used either to use the features or not, based on the requirement of the application.

As shown in [Figure 15.2](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_002), the selection of features is independent of any machine learning algorithm ([https://www.analyticsvidhya.com/blog/2016/12/introduction-to-feature-selection-methods-with-an-example-or-how-to-select-the-right-variables/](https://www.analyticsvidhya.com/blog/2016/12/introduction-to-feature-selection-methods-with-an-example-or-how-to-select-the-right-variables/)).

![Figure 15.2: Filter-based feature selection approach.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_002.png)

Figure 15.2: Filter-based feature selection approach.

### 15.3.2 Wrapper-based FS Methods

In the wrapper-based FS method, a feature subset is generated and is given as input to a learning model. Based on the output of the previous model, features can be added or removed; or a new feature subset can be formed based on some evaluation criteria. The problem is essentially reduced to a search problem. The flow of the method is shown in [Figure 15.3](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_003).

![Figure 15.3: Wrapper-based feature selection approach.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_003.png)

Figure 15.3: Wrapper-based feature selection approach.

### 15.3.3 Hybrid and embedded FS methods

The hybrid method is a mixture of filter and wrapper methods. Algorithms in embedded methods have an in-built FS mechanism. The most common examples are Lasso and Ridge regression techniques where they perform L1 and L2 regularization, respectively, to reduce overfitting.

### 15.3.4 Difference between filter-based and wrapper-based FS methods

While it is possible to use hybrid method that combines the characteristics of filter and wrapper, yet there are internal differences between filter-based and wrapper-based FS.

Such differences are mentioned in [Table 15.1](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_tab_001) concerning some specific characteristics.

Table 15.1:Difference between filter and wrapper FS method.

| S.No. || Characteristics || Filter-based FS method || Wrapper-based FS method |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. || Methodology || They measure the relevance of features by using their correlation with the dependent variable || They measure the usefulness of a subset of features by training a model on it. |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2. || Speed || Faster (as they do not involve training the model) || Comparatively slow |
| 3. || Computational cost || Computationally inexpensive || Computationally expensive |
| 4. || Evaluation methods used || Statistical methods || Cross-validation techniques |
| 5. || Accuracy || Inaccurate (Fails to find the best subset of features) || Accurate (Provides the best subset of features) |

## 15.4 An overview of evolutionary paradigm for feature selection

The diagram in [Figure 15.4](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_004) represents the taxonomy that classifies the evolutionary methods of FS as filter-based, wrapper-based, and hybrid-based. All the above-mentioned types of evolutionary methods can be classified based on the techniques followed such as evolutionary algorithm or swarm intelligence. The categories mentioned under each type like GA, GP under EA, and PSO, ACO under swarm intelligence are the basic interest of the chapter based on the recent research progresses in these areas.

![Figure 15.4: The proposed taxonomy of evolutionary methods for feature selection.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_004.png)

Figure 15.4: The proposed taxonomy of evolutionary methods for feature selection.

The upcoming sections explain some of the recent state-of-the-art research with the following combinations as mentioned below,

*   Filter-based GA (FB-GA), filter-based GP (FB-GP), filter-based PSO (FB-PSO), and filter-based ACO (FB-ACO).
    
*   Wrapper-based GA (WB-GA), wrapper-based GP (WB-GP), wrapper-based PSO (WB-PSO), and wrapper-based ACO (WB-ACO).
    
*   Hybrid-based GA (HB-GA), hybrid-based GP (HB-GP), hybrid-based PSO (HB-PSO), and hybrid-based ACO (HB-ACO).
    

## 15.5 Filter-based evolutionary methods of feature selection

The following subsections explain the recent research progresses in filter-based mechanism executed in combination with GA, GP, PSO, and ACO evolutionary approaches.

### 15.5.1 FB-GA

The initialization and the operations in the evolutionary approach of genetic algorithms are modified by introducing the concept of grouping the individuals as tribes. It is ensured that the features selected in each tribe follow Gaussian distribution. The exploration ability of individual tribes is compared to determine the competing tribes that are allowed to produce more individuals to search their respective solution space effectively. The proposed algorithm is compared with many existing FS approaches and is experimentally determined to produce accurate pattern classification ([Ma and Xia, 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_028)). FS that provides maximum discriminating ability using a fine level of information is proposed. A binary genetic algorithm is used with rough set concept for the above-mentioned feature granulation ([Dong et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_014)).

### 15.5.2 FB-GP

The genetic programming approach based on parsimony pressure is used for figure-ground segmentation as a preprocessing step in the image processing task. The experimental results of the proposed work are compared with many existing FS mechanisms and have shown improvement in F1-score ([Liang et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_025)). A new FS method is proposed to perform symbolic regression for high-dimensional data. The main objective of the system is to show improvement in the generalization ability using the regression task ([Chen et al., 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_008)). Automatic FS using image descriptor is proposed. GP has been utilized to synthesize a descriptor. Experiments have been conducted for classifying the image texture with different rotational degrees and compared with domain expert knowledge to assess the proposed system effectiveness ([Al-Sahaf et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_003)).

### 15.5.3 FB-PSO

A filter-based FS with a variant of PSO embedded with the GA is proposed to mitigate the premature convergence problem that exists in the conventional PSO approach. The work uses non-replaceable memory, secondary swarm, and a new update mechanism in velocity. In-depth analysis is performed for recognizing seven facial emotions by using multiple classifiers ([Mistry et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_030)). When exhaustive search does not result in the effective selection of features due to time-consumption, the use of a meta-heuristic PSO algorithm serves good in efficient search of the solution space. The proposed work has performed filter-based FS where the performance of the classifier and the selected features length are adopted as heuristic information. The experiments are conducted on benchmark data sets ([Aghdam and Heidari, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_001)).

### 15.5.4 FB-ACO

Novel selection of features is performed using a combination of graph clustering and ACO. One of the algorithms for detecting the communities is used after performing clustering and as a third step, ACO is used as a search paradigm to select the final subset of features. The evaluation is performed using the separability index and executed as a filter-based approach ([Moradi and Rostami, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_032)). A modified version of binary-coded ACO is combined with GA to perform filter-based FS. The proposed works have modeled on two aspects, one based on visibility density and the other based on pheromone density. The performance is analyzed by comparing the other existing and leading methods based on GA and PSO, and it is found that the proposed approach is better in terms of accuracy ([Wan et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_047)).

## 15.6 Wrapper-based evolutionary methods

The following subsections explain the recent research progresses in wrapper-based mechanism executed in combination with GA, GP, PSO, and ACO evolutionary approaches.

### 15.6.1 WB-GA

A bi-objective genetic algorithm is used for selecting the features where rough set and information theories are used for the definition of the objective function. A parallel implementation of ensemble FS is performed to reduce the execution time by using the pareto-based approach ([Das et al., 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_010)). Credit scoring in financial computation is handled using two methods namely information gain-based and wrapper-based genetic algorithm where for assessing the features K-nearest neighbor, Naive Bayes, and support vector machine classifiers are used. The observation from the results implies that classification accuracy mostly depends on the nature of the dataset used ([Jadhav et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_023)). To differentiate between normal and abnormal traffic, an anomaly-oriented intrusion detection system is proposed using the genetic algorithm and support vector machine. The new form of FS that uses the genetic algorithm makes a difference in terms of the fitness function used. More stable features are selected which have effectively reduced the false positive rate ([Gharaee and Hosseinvand, 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_016)). The work proposes a multi-stage FS mechanism to select the best parameters based on the time-frequency domain. GA is used in a supervised environment for selecting the best features along with the artificial neural network as a classifier. The exploration and exploitation abilities of the genetic algorithms are studied in the experiment conducted. The whole of the work is dedicated to condition-based monitoring of gearboxes ([Cerrada et al., 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_006)).

### 15.6.2 WB-GP

GP is used for powerful FS to improve the performance of segmentation in the standard classification process. The work has proposed three FS techniques that are tested using standard classifiers and are executed on two high varying datasets. Investigations of single and multiple objectives GP techniques have been performed ([Liang et al., 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_026)). An ensemble way of genetic programming is proposed for effective FS where the c-class problem is divided into c binary classification problem. A weighted voting scheme has been adopted in the ensemble that uses false-positive and false-negative values on the training data. The work is compared with a bi-objective GP scheme that uses a reduction of rule size strategy and the proposed method holds good ([Nag and Pal, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_033)).

### 15.6.3 WB-PSO

A new wrapper-based FS is proposed to improve the capability of a classifier to classify datasets which are incomplete. PSO is used for searching the feature subsets and C4.5 is used as the classifier to assess the feature subsets. Empirical results are obtained and analyzed on 14 datasets (Tran C.T. et al., 2016). A variant of PSO named as competitive swarm optimizer is used for resolving FS for high-dimensional problems. Here, the developed algorithm is used as a combinatorial optimization technique rather than being used as a continuous optimization technique. To reduce the cost of computation, an archive technique is also developed ([Gu et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_018)). PSO-based FS is performed with local search combined with *gbest* resetting mechanism to find the solution for local optima problem. The proposed algorithm is compared with three existing FS techniques. To ensure good generalization ability, it is noticed from the developed system that bias in FS should be avoided ([Tran et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_042)). For effective retrieval and analysis of an image, the semantic concepts should be assigned to an input image. An improved quantum-based PSO for selecting the features and ensemble-based boosting technique has been used in the research ([Jin and Jin, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_024)).

### 15.6.4 WB-ACO

The proposed work selects the optimal features without the use of prior information using ACO for searching and one classifier for assessing the features selected. The development of a facial recognition system is the application proposed in the work ([Dadaneh et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_009)). Wrapper-based approach in selecting the features is executed for the application of intrusion detection. The work uses support vector machine for validating the reduced data set. On performing the experiments on *KDD99* dataset, the detection based on signature and anomaly is intended to be performed ([Mehmod and Rais, 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_029)).

## 15.7 Hybrid-based evolutionary methods

The following subsections explain the recent research progresses in hybrid based mechanism executed in combination with GA, GP, PSO- and ACO evolutionary approaches.

### 15.7.1 HB-GA

To show improvement in randomization and search operation, the genetic algorithm is enhanced with new crossover and mutation operations. The comparison between the GA and the proposed enhanced GA results in a reduction in dimensions and time. Accurate categorization of text is performed by bringing forth some hybrid FS methods along with the enhanced genetic algorithm concept ([Ghareb et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_017)). Owing to several limitations that exist in the existing FS techniques, a hybrid GA that combines wrapper and embedded FS methods is proposed. A novel chromosome encoding is used where the objective is to optimize the control parameters for good optimization ([Liu et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_027)). A hybrid GA for efficient local search is proposed that works in combination with K-nearest neighbor classifier. The features are ranked and associated weight is given to those features during the classification process. The system is proposed to be applied for bringing innovation in management during the strategic decision making process ([Eroglu and Kilic, 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_015)).

### 15.7.2 HB-GP

High-dimensional feature construction and selection is executed to produce smaller feature sets that will improve the performance of the learning algorithm. A hybrid approach is followed and is applied to six high-dimensional data sets to prove the efficiency of the proposed work. Dynamically tuned evolutionary GP approaches have boosted the performance of the system (Tran et al., 2016, December).

### 15.7.3 HB-PSO

Correlation information-based local search strategy is used with the existing PSO concept to guide the search process effectively. The proposed work also determines the size of the feature subset to select the subset with a reduced size. The comparison with the existing state-of-the-art filter-based FS methods has been performed to show the effectiveness of the proposed hybrid strategy ([Moradi and Gholampour, 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_031)). A hybrid FS strategy is first proposed where most of the evaluations in the search process of PSO are filter-based and some of the evaluations are wrapper-based. The second proposal includes reducing the number of evaluations for wrapper-approach in order to save time. The proposed work depicts that the wrapper-approach results in achieving high classification performance with a reasonable number of selected features and the filter-approach results in good accuracy with a reduced number of features during the selection process ([Butler-Yeoman et al., 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_004)).

### 15.7.4 HB-ACO

ACO is used both for FS and classification by using the Ant-Miner classifier. The proposed hybrid method of FS performs well in comparison with other existing methods to discriminate the input features properly and in the optimal way of selecting the features ([Rasmy et al., 2012](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_038)). An ensemble classifier is coupled with ACO algorithm to perform effective FS and to improve the predictive accuracy of filters. Experiments are conducted on 11 publicly available data sets operated over four different classifiers. The results show that the proposed hybrid approach outperforms many existing FS techniques ([Naseer et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_034)).

## 15.8 Medical and other general surveys on feature selection (includes GA, GP, PSO, and ACO)

To detect arrhythmia in heart patients using ECG signals, a novel three-layer genetic algorithm-based classifier is built. The developed method combines the advantages of ensemble learning, deep learning, and evolutionary computation (EC). The experiments conducted by using the developed method have shown good classifier accuracy along with better recognition sensitivity in detecting the disease ([Pławiak and Acharya, 2019](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_036)). The parallel processes of FS along with parameter optimization of the artificial neural network have been designed using the GA for automatic diagnosis of breast cancer in women. The proposed method uses three types of back-propagation techniques for the effective diagnosis of the disease. The resilient back-propagation approaches of the proposed algorithm have produced the best results experimentally ([Ahmad F and Isa, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_002)). Mutation operation is applied aggressively in the GA to perform FS to design the brain–computer interface (BCI). The proposed FS is compared with the existing FS techniques that apply to BCI and the method is found to be superior ([Rejer, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_039)). A multi-objective system is proposed for intrusion detection where the problem of imbalanced classification is handled effectively. Method of special domination and predefined search based on multiple targets are the two strategies used. The work consists of bias-selection and fit-selection processes to handle the imbalanced data set to perform efficient FS ([Zhu et al., 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_050)). A rough set theory based FS is performed using a group incremental approach where the genetic algorithm is used for selecting optimal features. The objective is to perform FS dynamically by using the *reduct* set that is obtained in the previous generation in the optimization process ([Das et al., 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_011)). A tree-based embedded approach can be used for feature construction and selecting the features implicitly. The proposed work is used for high-dimensional data classification. Experimental investigations have been performed on seven high-dimensional gene expression problems and analyses of overfitting occurrence also have been handled (Tran et al., 2016, March). In the gene selection process to identify cancer, k-means clustering is used for redundancy removal and random forest is used for ranking the genes inside every cluster. The filtered genes are used in the classification process where eight benchmark classification methods are used. The filtered genes are also given as input to PSO + boosted C5.0 to compare the performance of the combined approach with eight benchmark classified output ([Pashaei et al., 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_035)). FS, which is an NP-hard problem, is performed on liver and kidney datasets to be used in the intelligent diagnosis system of diseases. Two modified PSO algorithms named velocity bounded and improved velocity bounded PSO algorithms are proposed to be used in the diagnosis system. The results of the new two algorithms are compared with the existing Boolean PSO algorithm and found to be comparatively better in selecting the features for the mentioned data sets ([Gunasundari et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_019)). To help pathologists in the diagnosis of breast cancer, the proposed system performs bandwidth selection and other feature subset selection for the estimation of kernel density in the decision making process. The work combines PSO with a kernel density estimator based classifier to perform the diagnosis. The objective function designed is used to show improvement in classification performance and the number of features selected ([Sheikhpour et al., 2016](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_041)). Ant activities have shown its importance in resolving several problems based on combinatorial optimization. Several filter-based iterations help in finding the optimal subset of features. To remove redundancy, feature relevance is used to find feature similarity. The output of FS is evaluated using the SVM classifier and the accuracy comparison between the whole set of features and selected features are performed ([Sabeena and Sarojini, 2015](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_040)). The search capability is improved using the probability method of encoding, hybrid operator and domination in Pareto relationship. The proposed cost-based multi-objective approach of FS is compared with other multi-objective algorithms for FS. The experiments are conducted on five benchmark datasets ([Zhang et al., 2017](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_049)).

## 15.9 Application of filter-based and wrapper-based PSO for health care informatics

### 15.9.1 Filter-based feature selection using a variant of PSO with digital pheromone concept

As the basic PSO method usually suffers from the local optima problem, the proposed variant of PSO uses digital pheromone as a third important component in the velocity update. This proposed methodology is executed as a filter-based model for the FS process ([Ramaswamy and Mukherjee, 2019](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_037)). The decrease in the computation time and increase in the classifier accuracy of the proposed FFS-PSODP have motivated to conduct the experimental results on four benchmark datasets taken from the UCI machine learning repository ([https://archive.ics.uci.edu/ml/datasets.php](https://archive.ics.uci.edu/ml/datasets.php)). New fitness functions were designed for handling imbalanced datasets. The performance analysis for diabetes disease in balanced data set and hepatitis disease in imbalanced data set are handled in the proposed work and shared as obtained below. The existing filter-based FS techniques that are considered for comparison are pair-wise correlation, Eigen-centrality, relief, and PSO. The proposed work has used the FFS-PSODP algorithm and has performed comparatively better in achieving good accuracy in possible minimum time.

The graph in [Figure 15.5](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_005) shows the performance comparison of the existing FS techniques with the proposed algorithm for balanced data set of diabetes disease.

![Figure 15.5: Performance comparison for diabetes disease.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_005.png)

Figure 15.5: Performance comparison for diabetes disease.

The graph in [Figure 15.6](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_006) shows the performance comparison for imbalanced data set of hepatitis disease.

![Figure 15.6: Performance comparison for hepatitis disease.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_006.png)

Figure 15.6: Performance comparison for hepatitis disease.

### 15.9.2 Wrapper-based feature selection using a variant of PSO with digital pheromone concept

The wrapper-based approach has implemented two FS mechanisms that use a variant of PSO with digital pheromone concept. Feed-forward neural network, back-propagation neural network and recurrent neural network are used in the classification process. The proposed novel approaches have given closely accurate results in minimum time ([Vanaja and Mukherjee, 2018](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_ref_046)). The performance comparison uses the existing FS techniques like pair-wise correlation, Eigen-centrality, latent, relief, and PSO where they have been used as a wrapper and compared with the proposed PSODP and PSO + PSODP techniques. The graphs in [Figures 15.7](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_007) and [15.8](#bodymatter-015.xhtml#b_9783110648195-015_fig_008) that uses the proposed two novel approaches have shown that for both breast cancer and chronic kidney disease data sets, they have performed comparatively better than the existing approaches.

![Figure 15.7: Performance comparison for breast cancer disease.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_007.png)

Figure 15.7: Performance comparison for breast cancer disease.

![Figure 15.8: Performance comparison for chronic kidney disease.](graphic/converted/b_9783110648195-015_fig_008.png)

Figure 15.8: Performance comparison for chronic kidney disease.

The performance is evaluated and shows the achievement of the two proposed methods in getting good accuracy in minimum time constraint.

## 15.10 Acknowledgement

The first author would like to thank the “Visvesvaraya PhD Scheme,” MeitY, New Delhi to support and appreciate the efforts financially in the form of scholarship.

## 15.11 Conclusion and future work

The chapter has contributed to reviewing some of the recent state-of-the-art research works in evolutionary approaches of FS. It is analyzed that in the past 5 years, there are reasonable FS approaches used with ACO and GP techniques. It is also noticed that there is number of research contributions for FS mechanism that have been used with GA and PSO. Owing to the importance of FS tasks in the machine learning cycle, it is believed that the research progression using evolutionary approaches of the search paradigm will be exponential in the mere future arena of computational intelligence. In particular, the contributions made in the health informatics domain have taken more importance when compared to other industries or applications. Thus, the interest of the chapter proposal has extended in explaining the implementation results that have been obtained through filter and wrapper approaches of FS using a variant of PSO to build an efficient clinical decision support system. As future plan, there are extended ideas to survey all the possible evolutionary approaches used for the FS process with regard to different time sensitive and time insensitive applications.
